class="infobox-data">2023&#160;estimate</td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div class="ib-country-fake-li">•&#160;Total</div></th><td class="infobox-data">$7.027&#160;billion<sup id="cite_ref-WEO2019_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WEO2019-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>&#32;(<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal)">153rd</a>)</td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div class="ib-country-fake-li">•&#160;Per capita</div></th><td <li value="1"><a href="/wiki/Montenegro_and_the_euro" title="Montenegro and the euro">Adopted unilaterally</a>; Montenegro is not a member of the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a>.</li>
</ol></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Montenegro</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;m&#39; in &#39;my&#39;">m</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;t&#39; in &#39;tie&#39;">t</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span>-<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/eɪ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;face&#39;">eɪ</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span>-<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ɛ/: &#39;e&#39; in &#39;dress&#39;">ɛ</span><span title="/ɡ/: &#39;g&#39; in &#39;guy&#39;">ɡ</span><span title="&#39;r&#39; in &#39;rye&#39;">r</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/0\/0b\/En-us-Montenegro.ogg\/En-us-Montenegro.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;En-us-Montenegro.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/0/0b/En-us-Montenegro.ogg/En-us-Montenegro.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Montenegro.ogg" title="File:En-us-Montenegro.ogg">i</a></sup></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">MON</span>-tih-<span style="font-size:90%">NEEG</span>-roh, -&#8288;<span style="font-size:90%">NAYG</span>-roh, -&#8288;<span style="font-size:90%">NEG</span>-roh</i></a>;<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>: <i lang="cnr">Crna Gora</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-native_name_1_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-native_name_1-10">&#91;a&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-native_name_2_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-native_name_2-11">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> <span title="Montenegrin-language text"><span lang="cnr">Црна Гора</span></span>,<sup id="cite_ref-native_name_3_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-native_name_3-12">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> <abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation"><a href="/wiki/Literal_translation" title="Literal translation">lit.</a></abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Black Mountain&#39;)<sup id="cite_ref-Vlada_Crne_Gore_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Vlada_Crne_Gore-13">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Влада_Црне_Горе_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Влада_Црне_Горе-14">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> is a country in <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Europe" title="Southeast Europe">Southeast Europe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> It is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Bosnia and Herzegovina">Bosnia and Herzegovina</a> to the northwest, <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a> to the northeast, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a> to the east, <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a> to the southeast, and <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic Sea</a> to the southwest with a coastline of 293.5&#160;km.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_Britannica_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia_Britannica-16">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Podgorica" title="Podgorica">Podgorica</a> (<a href="/wiki/Cyrillic_script" title="Cyrillic script">Cyrillic</a>: Подгорица) is the country's capital and its <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Montenegro" title="List of cities in Montenegro">largest city</a>, it covers 10.4% of Montenegro's territory of 13,812 square kilometres (5,333&#160;sq&#160;mi), and is home to roughly 31% of its total population of 621,000.<sup id="cite_ref-KÄ_Nationsonline.Org_1992_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KÄ_Nationsonline.Org_1992-17">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cetinje" title="Cetinje">Cetinje</a> (<a href="/wiki/Cyrillic_script" title="Cyrillic script">Cyrillic</a>: Цетиње) is the former royal capital and cultural centre  of Montenegro and is the location of several national institutions, including the <a href="/wiki/Blue_Palace" title="Blue Palace">official residence</a> of the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Montenegro" title="President of Montenegro">President of Montenegro</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>During the <a href="/wiki/Early_Medieval" class="mw-redirect" title="Early Medieval">Early Medieval</a> period, three principalities were located on the territory of modern-day Montenegro: <a href="/wiki/Duklja" title="Duklja">Duklja</a>, roughly corresponding to the southern half; <a href="/wiki/Travunia" title="Travunia">Travunia</a>, the west; and <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Serbia_(early_medieval)" title="Principality of Serbia (early medieval)">Rascia</a> proper, the north.<sup id="cite_ref-Luscombe_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luscombe-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sedlar_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sedlar-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Zeta_under_the_Bal%C5%A1i%C4%87i" title="Zeta under the Balšići">Principality of Zeta</a> emerged in the 14th and 15th centuries. From the late 14th century to the late 18th century, large parts of southern Montenegro were ruled by the <a href="/wiki/Venetian_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Venetian Republic">Venetian Republic</a> and incorporated into <a href="/wiki/Venetian_Albania" title="Venetian Albania">Venetian Albania</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> The name <i>Montenegro</i> was first used to refer to the country in the late 15th century. After falling under <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a> rule, Montenegro gained semi-autonomy in 1696 under the rule of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Petrović-Njegoš">House of Petrović-Njegoš</a>, first as a <a href="/wiki/Prince-Bishopric_of_Montenegro" title="Prince-Bishopric of Montenegro">theocracy</a> and later as a secular <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Montenegro" title="Principality of Montenegro">principality</a>. Montenegro's independence was recognised by the <a href="/wiki/Great_Powers" class="mw-redirect" title="Great Powers">Great Powers</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_Berlin" title="Congress of Berlin">Congress of Berlin</a> in 1878. In 1910, the country became a <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Montenegro" title="Kingdom of Montenegro">kingdom</a>. After <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, the kingdom became part of <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavia" title="Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a>. Following the <a href="/wiki/Breakup_of_Yugoslavia" title="Breakup of Yugoslavia">breakup of Yugoslavia</a>, the republics of <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Serbia_(1992%E2%80%932006)" title="Republic of Serbia (1992–2006)">Serbia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Montenegro_(1992%E2%80%932006)" title="Republic of Montenegro (1992–2006)">Montenegro</a> together proclaimed a <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">federation</a>. In June 2006 Montenegro declared its independence from <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">Serbia and Montenegro</a> following an <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_independence_referendum,_2006" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin independence referendum, 2006">independence referendum</a>, creating Montenegro and <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a> as they exist today.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Montenegro is therefore one of the newest internationally-recognised countries in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montenegro has an <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Montenegro" title="Economy of Montenegro">upper-middle-income economy</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-The_Heritage_Foundation_2021_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Heritage_Foundation_2021-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> and ranks 49th in the <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> It is a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Central_European_Free_Trade_Agreement" title="Central European Free Trade Agreement">Central European Free Trade Agreement</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-mvp.gov.me_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mvp.gov.me-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> Montenegro is also a founding member of the <a href="/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean" title="Union for the Mediterranean">Union for the Mediterranean</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-European_Western_Balkans_2020_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-European_Western_Balkans_2020-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> and has been in the process of <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_Montenegro_to_the_European_Union" title="Accession of Montenegro to the European Union">joining the European Union</a> since 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-European_Neighbourhood_Policy_And_Enlargement_Negotiations_-_European_Commission_2016_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-European_Neighbourhood_Policy_And_Enlargement_Negotiations_-_European_Commission_2016-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2><p>
The country's English name derives from a <a href="/wiki/Venetian_language" title="Venetian language">Venetian</a> <a href="/wiki/Calque" title="Calque">calque</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Slavic_languages" title="Slavic languages">Slavic</a> "Crna Gora", meaning "Black Mountain", deriving from the appearance of <a href="/wiki/Lov%C4%87en" title="Lovćen">Mount Lovćen</a> which was covered in dense evergreen forests.<sup id="cite_ref-visit-montenegro_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-visit-montenegro-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> <i>Crna Gora,</i> was mentioned for the first time in <a href="/wiki/Edict" title="Edict">edicts</a> issued by <a href="/wiki/Stefan_Uro%C5%A1_I" title="Stefan Uroš I">Stefan Uroš I</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church" title="Serbian Orthodox Church">Serbian Orthodox</a> <a href="/wiki/Zeta_(crown_land)" title="Zeta (crown land)">Zeta</a> <a href="/wiki/Bishop" title="Bishop">Episcopate</a> seat at <a href="/wiki/Vranjina_island" class="mw-redirect" title="Vranjina island">Vranjina island</a> in <a href="/wiki/Lake_Skadar" title="Lake Skadar">Lake Skadar</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> It came to denote the majority of contemporary Montenegro in the 15th century.<sup id="cite_ref-Fine532_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fine532-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> </p><figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg/220px-Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg/330px-Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg/440px-Doclea_Roman_city_Podgorica.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1597" data-file-height="1197" /></a><figcaption>Ruins of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_settlements_in_Illyria" title="List of settlements in Illyria">ancient city</a> of <a href="/wiki/Doclea_(Illyria)" title="Doclea (Illyria)">Doclea</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Modern-day Montenegro was more and more known by that name in the historical period following the fall of the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Despotate" title="Serbian Despotate">Serbian Despotate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Originally, it had referred to only a small strip of land under the rule of the <a href="/wiki/Pa%C5%A1trovi%C4%87i" title="Paštrovići">Paštrovići</a> tribe, but the name eventually came to be used for the wider mountainous region after the <a href="/wiki/Crnojevi%C4%87_noble_family" title="Crnojević noble family">Crnojević noble family</a> took power in <a href="/wiki/Upper_Zeta" title="Upper Zeta">Upper Zeta</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Fine532_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fine532-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> The aforementioned region became known as <i>Stara Crna Gora</i> '<a href="/wiki/Old_Montenegro" title="Old Montenegro">Old Montenegro</a>' by the 19th century to distinguish the independent region from the neighbouring Ottoman-occupied Montenegrin territory of <i>Brda</i> '(The) Highlands'. Montenegro further increased its size several times by the 20th century, as the result of wars against the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a>, which saw the annexation of <a href="/wiki/Old_Herzegovina" title="Old Herzegovina">Old Herzegovina</a> and parts of <a href="/wiki/Metohija" title="Metohija">Metohija</a> and southern <a href="/wiki/Ra%C5%A1ka_(region)" title="Raška (region)">Raška</a>. Its borders have changed little since then, losing <a href="/wiki/Metohija" title="Metohija">Metohija</a> and gaining the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Kotor" title="Bay of Kotor">Bay of Kotor</a>.
</p><p>After the second session of the <a href="/wiki/Anti-Fascist_Council_for_the_National_Liberation_of_Yugoslavia" title="Anti-Fascist Council for the National Liberation of Yugoslavia">AVNOJ</a> during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II_in_Yugoslavia" title="World War II in Yugoslavia">World War II in Yugoslavia</a>, the contemporary modern state of Montenegro was founded as the <i>Federal State of Montenegro</i> (<a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>: Савезна држава Црне Горе / <i>Savezna država Crne Gore</i>) on 15 November 1943 within the Yugoslav Federation by the <a href="/wiki/State_Anti-fascist_Council_for_the_National_Liberation_of_Montenegro_and_Boka" class="mw-redirect" title="State Anti-fascist Council for the National Liberation of Montenegro and Boka">ZAVNOCGB</a>. After the war, Montenegro became a republic under its name, the <i><a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Montenegro" title="Socialist Republic of Montenegro">People's Republic of Montenegro</a></i> (<a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>: Народна Република Црна Гора / <i>Narodna Republika Crna Gora</i>) on 29 November 1945. In 1963, it was renamed to the <i><a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Montenegro" title="Socialist Republic of Montenegro">Socialist Republic of Montenegro</a></i>  (<a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>: Социјалистичка Република Црна Гора / <i>Socijalistička Republika Crna Gora</i>). As the <a href="/wiki/Breakup_of_Yugoslavia" title="Breakup of Yugoslavia">breakup of Yugoslavia</a> occurred, the SRCG was renamed to the <i>Republic of Montenegro</i> (<a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>: Република Црна Гора / <i>Republika Crna Gora</i>) on 27 April 1992 within the <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">Federal Republic of Yugoslavia</a> by removing the adjective "socialist" from the republic's title. Since 22 October 2007, a year after its independence, the name of the country became simply known as <i>Montenegro</i>. The country is known as <i>Mali i Zi</i> (lit. black mountain) in <a href="/wiki/Albanians_in_Montenegro" title="Albanians in Montenegro">Albanian</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> while it is known as Crna Gora in Serbian, <a href="/wiki/Bosniaks_of_Montenegro" title="Bosniaks of Montenegro">Bosnian</a> as well as in <a href="/wiki/Croats_of_Montenegro" title="Croats of Montenegro">Croatian</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Montenegro" title="History of Montenegro">History of Montenegro</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Antiquity">Antiquity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Antiquity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Illyria" title="Illyria">Illyria</a>, <a href="/wiki/Illyricum_(Roman_province)" title="Illyricum (Roman province)">Illyricum (Roman province)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dalmatia_(Roman_province)" title="Dalmatia (Roman province)">Dalmatia (Roman province)</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Praevalitana" title="Praevalitana">Praevalitana</a></div>
<p>Modern-day Montenegro was part of <a href="/wiki/Illyria" title="Illyria">Illyria</a> and populated by the Indo-European-speaking <a href="/wiki/Illyrians" title="Illyrians">Illyrians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Boardman629_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boardman629-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Illyrian_kingdom" title="Illyrian kingdom">Illyrian kingdom</a> was conquered by the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Republic" title="Roman Republic">Roman Republic</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Illyro-Roman_Wars" title="Illyro-Roman Wars">Illyro-Roman Wars</a> and the region was incorporated into the <a href="/wiki/Illyricum_(Roman_province)" title="Illyricum (Roman province)">province of Illyricum</a> (later <a href="/wiki/Dalmatia_(Roman_province)" title="Dalmatia (Roman province)">Dalmatia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Praevalitana" title="Praevalitana">Praevalitana</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Arrival_of_the_Slavs">Arrival of the Slavs</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Arrival of the Slavs">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Duklja" title="Duklja">Duklja</a>, <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Zeta" title="Principality of Zeta">Principality of Zeta</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tribes_of_Montenegro" title="Tribes of Montenegro">Tribes of Montenegro</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Konstantin_Bodin.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Konstantin_Bodin.jpg/220px-Konstantin_Bodin.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="290" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Konstantin_Bodin.jpg/330px-Konstantin_Bodin.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/e/eb/Konstantin_Bodin.jpg 2x" data-file-width="370" data-file-height="487" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Constantine_Bodin" title="Constantine Bodin">Constantine Bodin</a>, medieval King and the ruler of <a href="/wiki/Duklja" title="Duklja">Duklja</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Three principalities were located on the territory: <a href="/wiki/Duklja" title="Duklja">Duklja</a>, roughly corresponding to the southern half, <a href="/wiki/Travunia" title="Travunia">Travunia</a>, the west, and <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Serbia_(early_medieval)" title="Principality of Serbia (early medieval)">Raška</a>, the north.<sup id="cite_ref-Luscombe_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luscombe-19">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sedlar_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sedlar-20">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Duklja gained its independence from the Byzantine Roman Empire in 1042. Over the next few decades, it expanded its territory to neighbouring Rascia and Bosnia, and also became recognised as a kingdom. Its power started declining at the beginning of the 12th century. After King Bodin's death (in 1101 or 1108), civil wars ensued. Duklja reached its zenith under Vojislav's son, <a href="/wiki/Mihailo_I_of_Duklja" title="Mihailo I of Duklja">Mihailo</a> (1046–1081), and his grandson <a href="/wiki/Constantine_Bodin" title="Constantine Bodin">Constantine Bodin</a> (1081–1101).<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As the nobility fought for the throne, the kingdom was weakened, and by 1186, the territory of modern-day Montenegro became part of the state ruled by <a href="/wiki/Stefan_Nemanja" title="Stefan Nemanja">Stefan Nemanja</a> and was a part of various state formations ruled by the <a href="/wiki/Nemanji%C4%87_dynasty" title="Nemanjić dynasty">Nemanjić dynasty</a> for the next two centuries. After the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Empire" title="Serbian Empire">Serbian Empire</a> collapsed in the second half of the 14th century, the most powerful Zetan family, the <a href="/wiki/Bal%C5%A1i%C4%87" class="mw-redirect" title="Balšić">Balšićs</a>, became sovereigns of Zeta.
</p><p>By the 13th century, <i>Zeta</i> had replaced <i>Duklja</i> when referring to the realm. In the late 14th century, southern Montenegro (<a href="/wiki/Lordship_of_Zeta" class="mw-redirect" title="Lordship of Zeta">Zeta</a>) came under the rule of the <a href="/wiki/Bal%C5%A1i%C4%87_noble_family" title="Balšić noble family">Balšić noble family</a>, then the <a href="/wiki/Crnojevi%C4%87_noble_family" title="Crnojević noble family">Crnojević noble family</a>, and by the 15th century, Zeta was more often referred to as <i>Crna Gora</i>.
</p>

//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Petar_II_Petrovic-2.JPG/270px-Petar_II_Petrovic-2.JPG 2x" data-file-width="509" data-file-height="741" /></a></span></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flex"><div class="thumbcaption"><b>Left:</b> <a href="/wiki/Petar_I_Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" title="Petar I Petrović-Njegoš">Petar I Petrović-Njegoš</a> was the most popular spiritual and military leader from the <a href="/wiki/Petrovi%C4%87_dynasty" class="mw-redirect" title="Petrović dynasty">Petrović dynasty</a>. <br /><b>Right:</b> <a href="/wiki/Petar_II_Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" title="Petar II Petrović-Njegoš">Petar II Petrović-Njegoš</a> was a <a href="/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="List of rulers of Montenegro">Prince-Bishop</a> (<i>vladika</i>) of Montenegro and the national poet and philosopher. Oil painting of Njegoš as <i>vladika</i>, c. 1837</div></div></div></div>
<p>In 1421, Zeta was annexed to the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Despotate" title="Serbian Despotate">Serbian Despotate</a>, but after 1455, another noble family from Zeta, the <a href="/wiki/Crnojevi%C4%87" class="mw-redirect" title="Crnojević">Crnojevićs</a>, became sovereign rulers of the country, making it the last free monarchy of the Balkans before it fell to the <a href="/wiki/Ottomans" class="mw-redirect" title="Ottomans">Ottomans</a> in 1496, and got annexed to the <i><a href="/wiki/Sanjak" title="Sanjak">sanjak</a></i> of <a href="/wiki/Shkod%C3%ABr" title="Shkodër">Shkodër</a>. For a short time, Montenegro existed as a separate autonomous <i>sanjak</i> in 1514–1528 (<a href="/wiki/Sanjak_of_Montenegro" title="Sanjak of Montenegro">Sanjak of Montenegro</a>). Also, <a href="/wiki/Old_Herzegovina" title="Old Herzegovina">Old Herzegovina</a> region was part of <a href="/wiki/Sanjak_of_Herzegovina" title="Sanjak of Herzegovina">Sanjak of Herzegovina</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_modern_period">Early modern period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Early modern period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Prince-Bishopric_of_Montenegro" title="Prince-Bishopric of Montenegro">Prince-Bishopric of Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Venetian_Albania" title="Venetian Albania">Venetian Albania</a></div>
<p>From 1392, numerous parts of the territory were controlled by <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Republic of Venice</a>, including the city of <a href="/wiki/Budva" title="Budva">Budva</a>, in that time known as "Budua". 
The Venetian territory was centred on the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Kotor" title="Bay of Kotor">Bay of Kotor</a>, and the Republic introduced governors who meddled in Montenegrin politics. Venice controlled territories in present-day Montenegro until <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_the_Republic_of_Venice" title="Fall of the Republic of Venice">its fall</a> in 1797.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-22">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
Large portions fell under the control of the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Empire" title="Ottoman Empire">Ottoman Empire</a> from 1496 to 1878. In the 16th century, Montenegro developed a unique form of autonomy within the Ottoman Empire that permitted Montenegrin clans freedom from certain restrictions. Nevertheless, the Montenegrins were disgruntled with Ottoman rule, and in the 17th century, repeatedly rebelled, which culminated in the defeat of the Ottomans in the <a href="/wiki/Great_Turkish_War" title="Great Turkish War">Great Turkish War</a> at the end of that century.
</p><p>Montenegrin territories were controlled by warlike clans. Most clans had a chieftain (<i>knez</i>), who was not permitted to assume the title unless he proved to be as worthy a leader as his predecessor. An assembly of Montenegrin clans (<i>Zbor</i>) was held every year on 12 July in Cetinje, and any adult clansman could take part.<sup id="cite_ref-Visit_Montenegro_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Visit_Montenegro-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In 1515, Montenegro became a <a href="/wiki/Theocracy" title="Theocracy">theocracy</a> led by the <a href="/wiki/Metropolitanate_of_Montenegro_and_the_Littoral" title="Metropolitanate of Montenegro and the Littoral">Metropolitanate of Montenegro and the Littoral</a>, which flourished after the <a href="/wiki/Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" class="mw-redirect" title="Petrović-Njegoš">Petrović-Njegoš</a> of Cetinje became the prince-bishop (whose title was "Vladika of Montenegro").
</p><p>People from Montenegro in this historical period were described as Orthodox Serbs.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Principality_and_Kingdom_of_Montenegro">Principality and Kingdom of Montenegro</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Principality and Kingdom of Montenegro">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Montenegro" title="Principality of Montenegro">Principality of Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Montenegro" title="Kingdom of Montenegro">Kingdom of Montenegro</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png/220px-Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="290" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png/330px-Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png/440px-Crna_Gora_-_Oslobodjenje_od_strane_okupacije_1711-1918.png 2x" data-file-width="1903" data-file-height="2506" /></a><figcaption>Expansion of Montenegro from 1711 to 1918 within present borders</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1858, one of the major Montenegrin victories over the Ottomans occurred at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Grahovac" title="Battle of Grahovac">Battle of Grahovac</a>. <a href="/wiki/Mirko_Petrovi%C4%87_Njego%C5%A1" class="mw-redirect" title="Mirko Petrović Njegoš">Grand Duke Mirko Petrović</a>, elder brother of <a href="/wiki/Knjaz_Danilo" class="mw-redirect" title="Knjaz Danilo">Knjaz Danilo</a>, led an army of 7,500 and defeated the numerically superior Ottomans with 15,000 troops at <a href="/wiki/Grahovac" title="Grahovac">Grahovac</a> on 1 May 1858. This forced the <a href="/wiki/Great_Powers" class="mw-redirect" title="Great Powers">Great Powers</a> to officially demarcate the borders between Montenegro and Ottoman Empire, <i>de facto</i> recognizing Montenegro's independence.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do,_Orao,_1877.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do%2C_Orao%2C_1877.jpg/200px-Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do%2C_Orao%2C_1877.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="153" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do%2C_Orao%2C_1877.jpg/300px-Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do%2C_Orao%2C_1877.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do%2C_Orao%2C_1877.jpg/400px-Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do%2C_Orao%2C_1877.jpg 2x" data-file-width="784" data-file-height="600" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do" title="Battle of Vučji Do">Battle of Vučji Do</a>, 1876, major battle of the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin%E2%80%93Ottoman_War_(1876%E2%80%931878)" title="Montenegrin–Ottoman War (1876–1878)">Montenegrin-Ottoman War</a></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg/200px-Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="138" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg/300px-Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg/400px-Kralj_i_kraljica_u_krugu_sire_familije.jpg 2x" data-file-width="928" data-file-height="638" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1_dynasty" title="Petrović-Njegoš dynasty">Royal family of Montenegro</a>: King <a href="/wiki/Nikola_I_Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" class="mw-redirect" title="Nikola I Petrović-Njegoš">Nicholas I</a> with his family including his grandson <a href="/wiki/Alexander_I_of_Yugoslavia" title="Alexander I of Yugoslavia">Alexander I. Karađorđević</a>, future King of Serbia and Yugoslavia. The <a href="/wiki/Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1_dynasty" title="Petrović-Njegoš dynasty">Petrović-Njegoš dynasty</a> ruled Montenegro continuously for almost 200 years.</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Vu%C4%8Dji_Do" title="Battle of Vučji Do">Battle of Vučji Do</a> Montenegrins inflicted a major defeat on the Ottoman Army under Grand Vizier <a href="/wiki/Ahmed_Muhtar_Pasha" title="Ahmed Muhtar Pasha">Ahmed Muhtar Pasha</a>. In the aftermath of the Russian victory against the Ottoman Empire in the <a href="/wiki/Russo-Turkish_War_of_1877%E2%80%931878" class="mw-redirect" title="Russo-Turkish War of 1877–1878">Russo-Turkish War of 1877–1878</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Great_powers_of_Europe" class="mw-redirect" title="Great powers of Europe">major powers</a> restructured the map of the Balkan region. The Ottoman Empire recognised the independence of Montenegro in the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Berlin_(1878)" title="Treaty of Berlin (1878)">Treaty of Berlin</a> in 1878.
</p><p>The first Montenegrin constitution (also known as the Danilo Code) was proclaimed in 1855. Under <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_I_of_Montenegro" title="Nicholas I of Montenegro">Nicholas I</a> (ruled 1860–1918), the <a href="/wiki/Principality" title="Principality">principality</a> was enlarged several times in the Montenegro-Turkish Wars and was recognised as independent in 1878. Nicholas I established diplomatic relations with the Ottoman Empire.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (October 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Minor border skirmishes excepted, diplomacy ushered in about 30 years of peace between the two states until the deposition of <a href="/wiki/Abdul_Hamid_II" title="Abdul Hamid II">Abdul Hamid II</a> in 1909.<sup id="cite_ref-ttk.gov.tr_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ttk.gov.tr-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The political skills of Abdul Hamid II and Nicholas I played a major role in the mutually amicable relations.<sup id="cite_ref-ttk.gov.tr_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ttk.gov.tr-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Modernization of the state followed, culminating with the draft of a Constitution in 1905. However, political rifts emerged between the reigning <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Party_of_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="People&#39;s Party of Montenegro">People's Party</a>, who supported the process of democratisation and union with Serbia, and those of the <a href="/wiki/True_People%27s_Party" title="True People&#39;s Party">True People's Party</a>, who were monarchist.
</p><p>In 1910, Montenegro became <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Montenegro" title="Kingdom of Montenegro">a kingdom</a>, and as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Balkan_Wars" title="Balkan Wars">Balkan Wars</a> of 1912–1913, a common border with Serbia was established, with <a href="/wiki/Shkod%C3%ABr" title="Shkodër">Shkodër</a> being awarded to <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a>, though the current capital city of Montenegro, Podgorica, was on the old border of Albania and Yugoslavia. Montenegro became one of the <a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_I" title="Allies of World War I">Allied Powers</a> during World War I (1914–18). In the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Mojkovac" title="Battle of Mojkovac">Battle of Mojkovac</a> fought in January 1916 between <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a> and the Kingdom of Montenegro, Montenegrins achieved a decisive victory even though they were outnumbered five to one. The Austro-Hungarians accepted military surrender on 25 January 1916.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPopeWhealRobbins2003431_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPopeWhealRobbins2003431-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> From 1916 to October 1918 Austria-Hungary occupied Montenegro. During the occupation, King Nicholas fled the country and established a <a href="/wiki/Government_in_exile" title="Government in exile">government-in-exile</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bordeaux" title="Bordeaux">Bordeaux</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia">Kingdom of Yugoslavia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Kingdom of Yugoslavia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia" title="Kingdom of Yugoslavia">Kingdom of Yugoslavia</a></div>
<p>In 1922, Montenegro formally became the <a href="/wiki/Zeta_Oblast" class="mw-redirect" title="Zeta Oblast">Oblast of Cetinje</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbs,_Croats_and_Slovenes" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Serbs, Croats and Slovenes">Kingdom of Serbs, Croats and Slovenes</a>, with the addition of the coastal areas around <a href="/wiki/Budva" title="Budva">Budva</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Kotor" title="Bay of Kotor">Bay of Kotor</a>. In a further restructuring in 1929, it became a part of a larger <a href="/wiki/Zeta_Banovina" title="Zeta Banovina">Zeta Banate</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia" title="Kingdom of Yugoslavia">Kingdom of Yugoslavia</a> that reached the <a href="/wiki/Neretva" title="Neretva">Neretva</a> River.
</p><p>Nicholas's grandson, the Serb King <a href="/wiki/Alexander_I_of_Yugoslavia" title="Alexander I of Yugoslavia">Alexander I</a>, dominated the Yugoslav government. <a href="/wiki/Zeta_Banovina" title="Zeta Banovina">Zeta Banovina</a> was one of nine banovinas that formed the kingdom; it consisted of the present-day Montenegro and parts of Serbia, Croatia, and Bosnia.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II_and_Socialist_Yugoslavia">World War II and Socialist Yugoslavia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: World War II and Socialist Yugoslavia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Italian_governorate_of_Montenegro" title="Italian governorate of Montenegro">Italian governorate of Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Montenegro" title="Socialist Republic of Montenegro">Socialist Republic of Montenegro</a></div>
<p>In April 1941, <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy" title="Kingdom of Italy">Kingdom of Italy</a>, and other Axis allies attacked and occupied the Kingdom of Yugoslavia. Italian forces occupied Montenegro and established a puppet <a href="/wiki/Italian_governorate_of_Montenegro" title="Italian governorate of Montenegro">Kingdom of Montenegro</a>.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-185-0116-27A,_Bucht_von_Kotor_(-),_jugoslawische_Schiffe.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-185-0116-27A%2C_Bucht_von_Kotor_%28-%29%2C_jugoslawische_Schiffe.jpg/200px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-185-0116-27A%2C_Bucht_von_Kotor_%28-%29%2C_jugoslawische_Schiffe.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="129" class="mw-file-element" Navy">Yugoslav Navy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Kotor" title="Bay of Kotor">Bay of Kotor</a> 1941</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg/200px-Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="184" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg/300px-Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg/400px-Osnivanje_Osme_crnogorske_brigade.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1480" data-file-height="1364" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">Great People's Assembly on the occasion of the establishment of the Eighth Montenegrin Brigade in <a href="/wiki/Berane" title="Berane">Berane</a>, February 25, 1944.</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg/200px-Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="137" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg/300px-Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3a/Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg/400px-Otvaranje_barske_pruge_28_maj_1976.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="547" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">Opening of <a href="/wiki/Belgrade%E2%80%93Bar_railway" title="Belgrade–Bar railway">Belgrade–Bar railway</a>. Construction of the line started in the 1950s and completed in 1976. The line was opened in 1976 by the Yugoslavian President <a href="/wiki/Josip_Broz_Tito" title="Josip Broz Tito">Josip Broz Tito</a>.</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>In May, the Montenegrin branch of the Communist Party of Yugoslavia started preparations for an uprising planned for mid-July. The Communist Party and its Youth League organised 6,000 of its members into detachments prepared for <a href="/wiki/Guerrilla_warfare" title="Guerrilla warfare">guerrilla warfare</a>. According to some historians, the first armed uprising in <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi</a>-occupied Europe happened on 13 July 1941 in Montenegro.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unexpectedly, the uprising took hold, and by 20 July, 32,000 men and women had joined the fight. Except for the coast and major towns (Podgorica, Cetinje, Pljevlja, and Nikšić), which were besieged, Montenegro was mostly liberated. In a month of fighting, the Italian army suffered 5,000 dead, wounded, and captured. The uprising lasted until mid-August, when it was suppressed by a counter-offensive of 67,000 Italian troops brought in from Albania. Faced with new and overwhelming Italian forces, many of the fighters laid down their arms and returned home. Nevertheless, intense guerrilla fighting lasted until December.
</p><p>Fighters who remained under arms fractured into two groups. Most of them went on to join the Yugoslav Partisans, consisting of communists and those inclined towards active resistance; these included <a href="/wiki/Arso_Jovanovi%C4%87" title="Arso Jovanović">Arso Jovanović</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sava_Kova%C4%8Devi%C4%87_(Yugoslav_Partisan)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sava Kovačević (Yugoslav Partisan)">Sava Kovačević</a>, <a href="/wiki/Svetozar_Vukmanovi%C4%87-Tempo" class="mw-redirect" title="Svetozar Vukmanović-Tempo">Svetozar Vukmanović-Tempo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Milovan_%C4%90ilas" class="mw-redirect" title="Milovan Đilas">Milovan Đilas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Peko_Dap%C4%8Devi%C4%87" title="Peko Dapčević">Peko Dapčević</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vlado_Dap%C4%8Devi%C4%87" title="Vlado Dapčević">Vlado Dapčević</a>, <a href="/wiki/Veljko_Vlahovi%C4%87" title="Veljko Vlahović">Veljko Vlahović</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bla%C5%BEo_Jovanovi%C4%87" title="Blažo Jovanović">Blažo Jovanović</a>. Those loyal to the <a href="/wiki/Kara%C4%91or%C4%91evi%C4%87_dynasty" title="Karađorđević dynasty">Karađorđević dynasty</a> and opposing communism went on to become <a href="/wiki/Chetniks" title="Chetniks">Chetniks</a>, and turned to collaboration with Italians against the Partisans.
</p><p>War broke out between Partisans and Chetniks during the first half of 1942. Pressured by Italians and Chetniks, the core of the Montenegrin Partisans went to Serbia and Bosnia, where they joined with other Yugoslav Partisans. Fighting between Partisans and Chetniks continued through the war. Chetniks with Italian backing controlled most of the country from mid-1942 to April 1943. Montenegrin Chetniks received the status of "anti-communist militia" and received weapons, ammunition, food rations, and money from Italy. Most of them were moved to <a href="/wiki/Mostar" title="Mostar">Mostar</a>, where they fought in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Neretva" class="mw-redirect" title="Battle of Neretva">Battle of Neretva</a> against the Partisans, but were dealt a heavy defeat.
</p><p>During German operation Schwartz against the Partisans in May and June 1943, Germans disarmed many Chetniks without fighting, as they feared they would turn against them in case of an Allied invasion of the Balkans. After the capitulation of Italy in September 1943, Partisans managed to take hold of most of Montenegro for a brief time, but Montenegro was soon <a href="/wiki/German_occupied_territory_of_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="German occupied territory of Montenegro">occupied by German forces</a>, and fierce fighting continued during late 1943 and 1944. Montenegro was liberated by the Partisans in December 1944.
</p><p>Montenegro became one of the six constituent republics of the communist <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" title="Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia</a> (SFRY). Its capital became Podgorica, renamed <a href="/wiki/Podgorica" title="Podgorica">Titograd</a> in honour of President <a href="/wiki/Josip_Broz_Tito" title="Josip Broz Tito">Josip Broz Tito</a>. After the war, the infrastructure of Yugoslavia was rebuilt, industrialization began, and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Montenegro" title="University of Montenegro">University of Montenegro</a> was established. Greater autonomy was established until the <a href="/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Montenegro" title="Socialist Republic of Montenegro">Socialist Republic of Montenegro</a> ratified a new constitution in 1974.<sup id="cite_ref-Roberts_1978_pp._136–146_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roberts_1978_pp._136–146-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Montenegro_within_FR_Yugoslavia">Montenegro within FR Yugoslavia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Montenegro within FR Yugoslavia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">Serbia and Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Montenegro_(1992%E2%80%932006)" title="Republic of Montenegro (1992–2006)">Republic of Montenegro (1992–2006)</a></div>
<p>After the dissolution of the SFRY in 1992, Montenegro remained part of a smaller <a href="/wiki/Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">Federal Republic of Yugoslavia</a> along with Serbia. In the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_independence_referendum,_1992" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin independence referendum, 1992">referendum on remaining in Yugoslavia in 1992</a>, the turnout was 66%, with 96% of the votes cast in favour of the federation with Serbia. The referendum was <a href="/wiki/Boycott" title="Boycott">boycotted</a> by the Muslim, Albanian, and Catholic minorities, as well as pro-independence Montenegrins. The opponents claimed that the poll was organised under anti-<a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democratic conditions</a> with widespread propaganda from the state-controlled media in favour of a pro-federation vote. No impartial report on the fairness of the referendum was made, as it was unmonitored, unlike in a later 2006 referendum when <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> observers were present.
</p><p>During the 1991–1995 <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_War" title="Bosnian War">Bosnian War</a> and <a href="/wiki/Croatian_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Croatian War">Croatian War</a>, Montenegrin police and military forces joined Serbian troops in attacks on <a href="/wiki/Dubrovnik" title="Dubrovnik">Dubrovnik</a>, Croatia.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> These operations, aimed at acquiring more territory, were characterised by large-scale violations of human rights.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG/290px-Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG" decoding="async" width="290" height="191" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG/435px-Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/89/Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG/580px-Former_Yugoslavia_2008.PNG 2x" data-file-width="1887" data-file-height="1242" /></a><figcaption>Map of the <a href="/wiki/Breakup_of_Yugoslavia" title="Breakup of Yugoslavia">disintegration of Yugoslavia</a> until 2008.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Montenegrin General <a href="/wiki/Pavle_Strugar" title="Pavle Strugar">Pavle Strugar</a> was convicted for his part in the bombing of Dubrovnik.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Bosnian refugees were arrested by Montenegrin police and transported to Serb camps in <a href="/wiki/Fo%C4%8Da_ethnic_cleansing" title="Foča ethnic cleansing">Foča</a>, where they were subjected to systematic torture and executed.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1996, <a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Milo Đukanović</a>'s government severed ties between Montenegro and its partner Serbia, which was led by <a href="/wiki/Slobodan_Milo%C5%A1evi%C4%87" title="Slobodan Milošević">Slobodan Milošević</a>. Montenegro formed its own <a href="/wiki/Economic_policy" title="Economic policy">economic policy</a> and adopted the German <a href="/wiki/Deutsche_Mark" title="Deutsche Mark">Deutsche Mark</a> as its currency and subsequently <a href="/wiki/Montenegro_and_the_euro" title="Montenegro and the euro">adopted the euro</a>, although not part of the <a href="/wiki/Eurozone" title="Eurozone">Eurozone</a>. Subsequent governments pursued pro-independence policies, and political tensions with Serbia simmered despite political changes in <a href="/wiki/Belgrade" title="Belgrade">Belgrade</a>.
</p><p>Targets in Montenegro were bombed by NATO forces during <a href="/wiki/Operation_Allied_Force" class="mw-redirect" title="Operation Allied Force">Operation Allied Force</a> in 1999, although the extent of these attacks was limited in both time and area affected.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2002, Serbia and Montenegro came to a new agreement for continued cooperation and entered into negotiations regarding the future status of the <a href="/wiki/Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia" class="mw-redirect" title="Federal Republic of Yugoslavia">Federal Republic of Yugoslavia</a>. This resulted in the Belgrade Agreement, which saw the country's transformation into a more decentralised state union named <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">Serbia and Montenegro</a> in 2003. The Belgrade Agreement also contained a provision delaying any future referendum on the independence of Montenegro for at least three years.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence">Independence</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Independence">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">Serbia and Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_independence_referendum,_2006" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin independence referendum, 2006">Montenegrin independence referendum, 2006</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/2016_Montenegrin_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat_attempt_allegations" class="mw-redirect" title="2016 Montenegrin coup d&#39;état attempt allegations">2016 Montenegrin coup d'état attempt allegations</a> and <a href="/wiki/2019%E2%80%9320_Montenegrin_crisis" class="mw-redirect" title="2019–20 Montenegrin crisis">2019–20 Montenegrin crisis</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Independent_montenegro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Independent_montenegro.jpg/220px-Independent_montenegro.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Independent_montenegro.jpg/330px-Independent_montenegro.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Independent_montenegro.jpg/440px-Independent_montenegro.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1067" /></a><figcaption>Supporters of Montenegrin independence in June 2006 in <a href="/wiki/Cetinje" title="Cetinje">Cetinje</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The status of the union between Montenegro and Serbia was decided by a <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_independence_referendum,_2006" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin independence referendum, 2006">referendum on Montenegrin independence</a> on 21 May 2006. A total of 419,240 votes were cast, representing 86.5% of the electorate; 230,661 votes (55.5%) were for independence and 185,002 votes (44.5%) were against.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> This narrowly surpassed the 55% threshold needed to validate the referendum under the rules set by the European Union. According to the electoral commission, the 55% threshold was passed by only 2,300 votes. Serbia, the member-states of the European Union, and the <a href="/wiki/Permanent_members_of_the_United_Nations_Security_Council" title="Permanent members of the United Nations Security Council">permanent members of the United Nations Security Council</a> all <a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_recognition" title="Diplomatic recognition">recognised</a> Montenegro's independence.
</p><p>The 2006 referendum was monitored by five international observer missions, headed by an <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">OSCE</a>/<a href="/wiki/Office_for_Democratic_Institutions_and_Human_Rights" title="Office for Democratic Institutions and Human Rights">ODIHR</a> team, and around 3,000 observers in total (including domestic observers from <a href="/wiki/Center_for_Democracy_and_Technology" title="Center for Democracy and Technology">CDT</a> (OSCE PA), the Parliamentary Assembly of the Council of Europe (PACE), the Congress of Local and Regional Authorities of the Council of Europe (CLRAE), and the European Parliament (EP) to form an International Referendum Observation Mission (IROM). The IROM—in its preliminary report—"assessed compliance of the referendum process with OSCE commitments, Council of Europe commitments, other international standards for democratic electoral processes, and domestic legislation." Furthermore, the report stated that the competitive pre-referendum environment was marked by an active and generally peaceful campaign and that "there were no reports of restrictions on fundamental civil and political rights."
</p><p>On 3 June 2006, the Montenegrin Parliament declared the independence of Montenegro,<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> formally confirming the result of the referendum.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Under_Secretary_Shannon_Poses_for_a_Photo_With_Montenegro%27s_Prime_Minister_Markovic,_Montenegro%27s_Foreign_Minister_Darmanovic,_and_NATO_Secretary-General_Stoltenberg_at_a_Ceremony_in_Washington_(34734796140).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Under_Secretary_Shannon_Poses_for_a_Photo_With_Montenegro%27s_Prime_Minister_Markovic%2C_Montenegro%27s_Foreign_Minister_Darmanovic%2C_and_NATO_Secretary-General_Stoltenberg_at_a_Ceremony_in_Washington_%2834734796140%29.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Under_Secretary_Shannon_Poses_for_a_Photo_With_Montenegro%27s_Prime_Minister_Markovic%2C_Montenegro%27s_Foreign_Minister_Darmanovic%2C_and_NATO_Secretary-General_Stoltenberg_at_a_Ceremony_in_Washington_%2834734796140%29.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Under_Secretary_Shannon_Poses_for_a_Photo_With_Montenegro%27s_Prime_Minister_Markovic%2C_Montenegro%27s_Foreign_Minister_Darmanovic%2C_and_NATO_Secretary-General_Stoltenberg_at_a_Ceremony_in_Washington_%2834734796140%29.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3789" data-file-height="2706" /></a><figcaption>Montenegro Foreign Minister Srdjan Darmanović presents a document to <a href="/wiki/US" class="mw-redirect" title="US">US</a> Undersecretary of State for Political Affairs <a href="/wiki/Thomas_A._Shannon_Jr." title="Thomas A. Shannon Jr.">Thomas Shannon</a> and Montenegro Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Du%C5%A1ko_Markovi%C4%87" title="Duško Marković">Duško Marković</a> during a ceremony at the <a href="/wiki/State_Department" class="mw-redirect" title="State Department">State Department</a> to accept Montenegro's instrument of accession to the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Treaty_Organization" class="mw-redirect" title="North Atlantic Treaty Organization">North Atlantic Treaty Organization</a> (NATO).</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 28 June 2006, Montenegro joined the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> as its 192nd member state.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montenegro has been dominated since the breakup of Yugoslavia by <a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Milo Đukanović</a> (four-time prime minister and also twice president), accused of having established an <a href="/wiki/Authoritarianism" title="Authoritarianism">authoritarianism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Clientelism" title="Clientelism">clientelist</a> regime, while maintaining close relations with <a href="/wiki/Organized_crime" title="Organized crime">organized crime</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The massive privatizations of the Đukanović era lead to the enrichment of him and oligarchs close to him. His brother Aleksandar, owner of Montenegro's first private bank, oversaw the privatizations, while his sister, Ana Kolarevic, has long controlled the judiciary. The clientelist networks of the ruling party dominated all segments of social life. A party card was required to start a business or obtain a position in the administration. This policy also contributed to the reinforcement of regional disparities and social inequalities. Unemployment climbs to 36.6 per cent in the northern part of the country, compared to 3.9 per cent in the coastal region, while a quarter of the population lives below the poverty line (2018).<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Law_on_the_Status_of_the_Descendants_of_the_Petrovi%C4%87_Njego%C5%A1_Dynasty" title="Law on the Status of the Descendants of the Petrović Njegoš Dynasty">Law on the Status of the Descendants of the Petrović Njegoš Dynasty</a> was passed by the Parliament of Montenegro on 12 July 2011. It rehabilitated the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Petrović-Njegoš">Royal House of Montenegro</a> and recognised limited symbolic roles within the constitutional framework of the republic.
</p><p>In 2015, the investigative journalists' network <a href="/wiki/Organized_Crime_and_Corruption_Reporting_Project" title="Organized Crime and Corruption Reporting Project">OCCRP</a> named Montenegro's long-time President and Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Milo Đukanović</a> "Person of the Year in Organized Crime".<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The extent of Đukanović's corruption led to street demonstrations and calls for his removal.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2016, for the day of the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_parliamentary_election,_2016" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin parliamentary election, 2016">parliamentary election</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_coup_d%27%C3%A9tat_attempt" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin coup d&#39;état attempt">coup d'état was prepared</a> by a group of persons that included leaders of the Montenegrin opposition, Serbian nationals and Russian agents; the coup was prevented.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, fourteen people, including two Russian nationals and two Montenegrin opposition leaders, <a href="/wiki/Andrija_Mandi%C4%87" title="Andrija Mandić">Andrija Mandić</a> and <a href="/wiki/Milan_Kne%C5%BEevi%C4%87_(Montenegrin_politician)" title="Milan Knežević (Montenegrin politician)">Milan Knežević</a>, were indicted for their alleged roles in the coup attempt on charges such as "preparing a conspiracy against the constitutional order and the security of Montenegro" and an "attempted terrorist act."<sup id="cite_ref-rferlcoup_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rferlcoup-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Recent_history">Recent history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Recent history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Protest_in_Kotor,_Jan_2020.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Protest_in_Kotor%2C_Jan_2020.jpg/220px-Protest_in_Kotor%2C_Jan_2020.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Protest_in_Kotor%2C_Jan_2020.jpg/330px-Protest_in_Kotor%2C_Jan_2020.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b9/Protest_in_Kotor%2C_Jan_2020.jpg/440px-Protest_in_Kotor%2C_Jan_2020.jpg 2x" data-file-width="969" data-file-height="720" /></a><figcaption>The controversial 2019 <a href="/wiki/2019-2020_clerical_protests_in_Montenegro#Background" class="mw-redirect" title="2019-2020 clerical protests in Montenegro">law on religious communities</a>, introduced by the former ruling <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Socialists_of_Montenegro" title="Democratic Party of Socialists of Montenegro">DPS</a>, proposed the transfer of the majority of religious objects and land owned by the largest religious organization in the country, the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church" title="Serbian Orthodox Church">SPC</a>, to the Montenegrin state. It sparked a <a href="/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_clerical_protests_in_Montenegro" title="2019–2020 clerical protests in Montenegro">series of massive protests</a> of the Serbian clerical right and pro-Serbian radical parties in Montenegro across the country, which led to the first <a href="/wiki/2020_Montenegrin_parliamentary_election" title="2020 Montenegrin parliamentary election">government change</a> in the country's history.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Montenegro formally <a href="/wiki/Montenegro%E2%80%93NATO_relations" title="Montenegro–NATO relations">became a member</a> of <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> in June 2017, despite attempts by Russia to sabotage it,<sup id="cite_ref-murkypolitico_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-murkypolitico-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> an event that triggered a promise of retaliatory actions from Russia's government.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montenegro has been in <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_Montenegro_to_the_European_Union" title="Accession of Montenegro to the European Union">negotiations</a> with the EU since 2012. In 2018, the earlier goal of acceding by 2022<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> was revised to 2025.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Legislation is being passed bringing Montenegro law in line with EU membership requirements.
</p><p>In April 2018, <a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Milo Djukanovic</a>, the leader of the ruling <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Socialists_of_Montenegro" title="Democratic Party of Socialists of Montenegro">Democratic Party of Socialists</a> (DPS), won Montenegro's <a href="/wiki/2018_Montenegrin_presidential_election" title="2018 Montenegrin presidential election">presidential election</a>. The veteran politician had served as Prime Minister six times and as president once before. He had dominated Montenegrin politics since 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/2019_Montenegrin_anti-corruption_protests" title="2019 Montenegrin anti-corruption protests">Anti-corruption protests</a> began in February 2019 against Đukanović and the Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Du%C5%A1ko_Markovi%C4%87" title="Duško Marković">Duško Marković</a>-led <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Montenegro#Marković_Cabinet_(2016-present)" title="Government of Montenegro">government</a> of the ruling <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Socialists" class="mw-redirect" title="Democratic Party of Socialists">Democratic Party of Socialists</a> (DPS), which had been in power since 1991.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of late&#32;December&#160;2019,<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> the newly adopted Law on Religion, which <a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a> transferred the ownership of church buildings and estates built before 1918 from the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church" title="Serbian Orthodox Church">Serbian Orthodox Church</a> to the Montenegrin state,<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> sparked large<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_clerical_protests_in_Montenegro" title="2019–2020 clerical protests in Montenegro">protests</a> and road blockages.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Seventeen opposition <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Front_(Montenegro)" title="Democratic Front (Montenegro)">Democratic Front</a> MPs were arrested prior to the voting for disrupting the vote.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Demonstrations continued into March<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> 2020 as peaceful protest walks, mostly organised by the <a href="/wiki/Metropolitanate_of_Montenegro_and_the_Littoral" title="Metropolitanate of Montenegro and the Littoral">Metropolitanate of Montenegro and the Littoral</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Eparchy_of_Budimlja_and_Nik%C5%A1i%C4%87" title="Eparchy of Budimlja and Nikšić">Eparchy of Budimlja and Nikšić</a> in the majority of Montenegrin municipalities.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In its political rights and civil liberties worldwide report in May 2020, <a href="/wiki/Freedom_House" title="Freedom House">Freedom House</a> marked Montenegro as a <a href="/wiki/Hybrid_regime" title="Hybrid regime">hybrid regime</a> rather than a democracy because of declining standards in governance, justice, elections, and media freedom.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> For the first time in three decades, in the <a href="/wiki/2020_Montenegrin_parliamentary_election" title="2020 Montenegrin parliamentary election">2020 parliamentary election</a>, the opposition won more votes than Đukanović's ruling party.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> In February 2022, that very same government was voted out in the first successful vote of no-confidence in the country's history.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2022 an investigation linked six Russian diplomats with twenty eight Russian citizens holding temporary visas for Montenegro and two local citizens in a spy investigation. The diplomats were expelled.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> The Russian citizens were later banned from Montenegro and the two locals, one an ex-diplomat, face charges of illegal weapons, organising a criminal organisation and espionage.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2023, <a href="/wiki/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87" title="Jakov Milatović">Jakov Milatovic</a>, a pro-western candidate of the <a href="/wiki/Europe_Now!" title="Europe Now!">Europe Now</a> movement, won the presidential <a href="/wiki/2023_Montenegrin_presidential_election" title="2023 Montenegrin presidential election">election</a> run-off over incumbent Milo Djukanovic to succeed him as the incumbent <a href="/wiki/President_of_Montenegro" title="President of Montenegro">president of Montenegro</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Europe_Now!" title="Europe Now!">Europe Now</a> movement won the highest number of seats in the <a href="/wiki/2023_Montenegrin_parliamentary_election" title="2023 Montenegrin parliamentary election">2023 Montenegrin parliamentary election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Montenegro" title="Geography of Montenegro">Geography of Montenegro</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Montenegro_Map.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Montenegro_Map.png/350px-Montenegro_Map.png" decoding="async" width="350" height="261" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Montenegro_Map.png/525px-Montenegro_Map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Montenegro_Map.png/700px-Montenegro_Map.png 2x" data-file-width="2427" data-file-height="1807" /></a><figcaption>Map of Montenegro.</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner"
