/></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Accursed_Mountains" title="Accursed Mountains">Prokletije National Park</a></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg/200px-Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg/300px-Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg/400px-Nacionalni_park_Skadarsko_Jezero_12.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3888" data-file-height="2592" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Lake_Skadar" title="Lake Skadar">Lake Skadar National Park</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspot" title="Biodiversity hotspot">hotspot</a> of <a href="/wiki/Freshwater_ecosystem" title="Freshwater ecosystem">freshwater biodiversity</a></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora,_Montenegro_02.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora%2C_Montenegro_02.jpg/200px-Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora%2C_Montenegro_02.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora%2C_Montenegro_02.jpg/300px-Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora%2C_Montenegro_02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora%2C_Montenegro_02.jpg/400px-Beauty_of_National_Park_Biogradska_Gora%2C_Montenegro_02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="7360" data-file-height="4912" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Biogradska_Gora" title="Biogradska Gora">Biogradska Gora National Park</a></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Montenegro features high peaks along its borders with Serbia, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>, and Albania, a segment of the <a href="/wiki/Karst" title="Karst">Karst</a> of the western Balkan Peninsula, to a narrow coastal plain that is only 1.5 to 6 kilometres (1 to 4 miles) wide. The plain stops abruptly in the north, where <a href="/wiki/Mount_Lov%C4%87en" class="mw-redirect" title="Mount Lovćen">Mount Lovćen</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mount_Orjen" class="mw-redirect" title="Mount Orjen">Mount Orjen</a> plunge into the inlet of the Bay of Kotor.
</p><p>Montenegro's large karst region lies generally at elevations of 1,000 metres (3,280&#160;ft) above sea level; some parts, however, rise to 2,000&#160;m (6,560&#160;ft), such as <a href="/wiki/Mount_Orjen" class="mw-redirect" title="Mount Orjen">Mount Orjen</a> (1,894&#160;m or 6,214&#160;ft), the highest massif among the coastal limestone ranges. The <a href="/wiki/Zeta_River" class="mw-redirect" title="Zeta River">Zeta River</a> valley, at an elevation of 500&#160;m (1,600&#160;ft), is the lowest segment.
</p><p>The mountains of Montenegro include some of the most rugged terrains in Europe, averaging more than 2,000 metres (6,600 feet) in elevation. One of the country's notable peaks is <a href="/wiki/Bobotov_Kuk" title="Bobotov Kuk">Bobotov Kuk</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Durmitor" title="Durmitor">Durmitor</a> mountains, which reaches a height of 2,522&#160;m (8,274&#160;ft). Owing to the hyperhumid climate on their western sides, the Montenegrin mountain ranges were among the most ice-eroded parts of the Balkan Peninsula during the last glacial period.
</p><p>Internationally, Montenegro borders <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Bosnia and Herzegovina">Bosnia and Herzegovina</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kosovo" title="Kosovo">Kosovo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a> and <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a>. It lies between latitudes <a href="/wiki/41st_parallel_north" title="41st parallel north">41°</a> and <a href="/wiki/44th_parallel_north" title="44th parallel north">44°N</a>, and longitudes <a href="/wiki/18th_meridian_east" title="18th meridian east">18°</a> and <a href="/wiki/21st_meridian_east" title="21st meridian east">21°E</a>.
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <p>Montenegro is a member of the <a href="/wiki/International_Commission_for_the_Protection_of_the_Danube_River" title="International Commission for the Protection of the Danube River">International Commission for the Protection of the Danube River</a>, as more than 2,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (772&#160;sq&#160;mi) of the country's territory lie within the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a> <a href="/wiki/Drainage_basin" title="Drainage basin">catchment area</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The diversity of the geological base, landscape, climate, and soil, and the position of Montenegro on the Balkan Peninsula and Adriatic Sea, created the conditions for high biological diversity, putting Montenegro among the "hot-spots" of European and world biodiversity. The number of species per area unit index in Montenegro is 0.837, the highest in any European country.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Biological estimates suggest that over 1,200 species of freshwater algae, 300 species of marine algae, 589 species of moss, 7,000–8,000 species of vascular plants, 2,000 species of fungi, 16,000–20,000 species of insects, 407 species of marine fish, 56 species of reptile, 333 species of regularly visiting birds and a high species diversity of mammals are found in Montenegro.<sup id="cite_ref-Home_&#124;_Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_1970_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Home_|_Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_1970-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montenegro can be divided into two main <a href="/wiki/Biogeography" title="Biogeography">biogeographic</a> regions, which include the <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_Biogeographic_Region" title="Mediterranean Biogeographic Region">Mediterranean Biogeographic Region</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Alpine_Biogeographic_Region" title="Alpine Biogeographic Region">Alpine Biogeographic Region</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Home_&#124;_Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_1970_92-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Home_|_Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_1970-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> It is also home to three terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Balkan_mixed_forests" title="Balkan mixed forests">Balkan mixed forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dinaric_Mountains_mixed_forests" title="Dinaric Mountains mixed forests">Dinaric Mountains mixed forests</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Illyrian_deciduous_forests" title="Illyrian deciduous forests">Illyrian deciduous forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> It had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 6.41/10, ranking it 73rd globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The total share of protected areas in Montenegro is 9.05% of the country's area, which mainly comes from the five <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Montenegro" title="List of national parks of Montenegro">national parks of Montenegro</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Home_&#124;_Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_1970_92-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Home_|_Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_1970-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Montenegro" title="Politics of Montenegro">Politics of Montenegro</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:249px;max-width:249px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:124px;max-width:124px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_(2023-05-30).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Milo Đukanović" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_%282023-05-30%29.jpg/122px-Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_%282023-05-30%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="122" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_%282023-05-30%29.jpg/183px-Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_%282023-05-30%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_%282023-05-30%29.jpg/244px-Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87_%282023-05-30%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="619" data-file-height="872" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87" title="Jakov Milatović">Jakov Milatović</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/President_of_Montenegro" title="President of Montenegro">President</a> (representative head of state)</small></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:121px;max-width:121px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_(cropped).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Dritan Abazović" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_%28cropped%29.jpg/119px-Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_%28cropped%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="119" height="171" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_%28cropped%29.jpg/179px-Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_%28cropped%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_%28cropped%29.jpg/238px-Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87_PM_%28cropped%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="753" data-file-height="1085" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87" title="Dritan Abazović">Dritan Abazović</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Montenegro" title="Prime Minister of Montenegro">Prime Minister</a> (head of government)</small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Since the breakup of the <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">political union of Serbia and Montenegro</a> in 2006, Montenegro has been an independent, <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a>. 
</p><p>Montenegro is a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">republic</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Montenegro" title="Constitution of Montenegro">codified constitution</a> established in 2007. The constitution describes Montenegro as a "<a href="/wiki/Civic_engagement" title="Civic engagement">civic</a>, democratic, <a href="/wiki/Environmentalism" title="Environmentalism">ecological</a> state of <a href="/wiki/Social_justice" title="Social justice">social justice</a>, based on the <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_law" title="Rule of law">reign of Law</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Montenegro is a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>. 
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Montenegro" title="President of Montenegro">President of Montenegro</a> is the representative <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>, elected for a period of five years through <a href="/wiki/Direct_election" title="Direct election">direct election</a>. The President promotes the country internationally through diplomatic engagements, promulgates laws by ordinance, calls elections for the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Montenegro" title="Parliament of Montenegro">Parliament</a>, and ceremonially proposes candidates for <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Montenegro" title="Prime Minister of Montenegro">Prime Minister</a>, president and justices of the Constitutional Court to the Parliament. The President also ceremonially proposes the calling of a <a href="/wiki/Referendum" title="Referendum">referendum</a> to Parliament, grants amnesty for criminal offences prescribed by the national law, confers decoration and awards and performs other constitutional duties and is a member of the Supreme Defence Council. The official residence of the President is in <a href="/wiki/Cetinje" title="Cetinje">Cetinje</a>. The incumbent president is <a href="/wiki/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87" title="Jakov Milatović">Jakov Milatović</a> who has held the position since 2023.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Montenegro" title="Government of Montenegro">Government of Montenegro</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive branch</a> of government authority of Montenegro and led by the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Montenegro" title="Prime Minister of Montenegro">Prime Minister</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> The role of Prime Minister is the most politically powerful office in Montenegro. All of Montenegro's governments since 2006 have been <a href="/wiki/Coalition_government" title="Coalition government">coalitions</a> comprising a minimum of three <a href="/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Montenegro" title="List of political parties in Montenegro">political parties.</a> The government is primarily based in <a href="/wiki/Podgorica" title="Podgorica">Podgorica</a>. 
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Montenegro" title="Parliament of Montenegro">Parliament of Montenegro</a> is the country's <a href="/wiki/Unicameral" class="mw-redirect" title="Unicameral">unicameral</a> <a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">legislature</a>, located in <a href="/wiki/Podgorica" title="Podgorica">Podgorica</a>. The Parliament has power to appoint the government, pass <a href="/wiki/Legislation" title="Legislation">legislation</a> (parliamentary law) and <a href="/wiki/Legislative_scrutiny" title="Legislative scrutiny">scrutinise</a> <a href="/wiki/Bill_(law)" title="Bill (law)">bills</a> (proposed parliamentary law). It also appoints justices of all courts, approves the budget and performs other duties as established by the country's Constitution. Parliament can pass a <a href="/wiki/Motion_of_no_confidence" title="Motion of no confidence">motion of no confidence</a> in the Government by <a href="/wiki/Simple_majority_vote" class="mw-redirect" title="Simple majority vote">simple majority vote</a>. One member of the Montenegrin parliament, known as a Deputy, is elected per 6,000 voters.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> There are currently 81 deputies. Elections to the Parliament are conducted by the <a href="/wiki/D%27Hondt_method" title="D&#39;Hondt method">D'Hondt method</a>, a form of <a href="/wiki/Proportional_representation" title="Proportional representation">proportional representation</a>.
</p><p>In 2019, Montenegro was described as a <a href="/wiki/Hybrid_regime" title="Hybrid regime">hybrid regime</a> (a political system which combines <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democractic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Authoritarian_government" class="mw-redirect" title="Authoritarian government">authoritarian</a> features) according to the <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>-based <a href="/wiki/Freedom_House" title="Freedom House">Freedom House</a>. The organisation cited <a href="/wiki/State_capture" title="State capture">state capture</a>, abuse of power, and strongman tactics by Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Milo Đukanović</a> (2008-2010 and 2012–2016) as explanations for its description as such. <a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Đukanović</a> held a number of senior government positions, including the Presidency, before and after the dissolution of the union between Serbia and Montenegro. In 2021, the <i><a href="/wiki/The_Economist_Democracy_Index" title="The Economist Democracy Index">Economist Democracy Index</a></i> (EDI) declared Montenegro as a "flawed democracy" and as of 2023,<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> it remains regarded as such by the EDI. 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Milo_%C4%90ukanovi%C4%87" title="Milo Đukanović">Đukanović</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Pro-Europeanism" title="Pro-Europeanism">pro-European</a> and <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">pro-NATO</a> <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_of_Socialists_of_Montenegro" title="Democratic Party of Socialists of Montenegro">Democratic Party of Socialists</a> (DPS) narrowly lost the <a href="/wiki/2020_Montenegrin_parliamentary_election" title="2020 Montenegrin parliamentary election">2020 parliamentary election</a> which ended the party's 30-year rule<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> and the pro-Serbia "<a href="/wiki/For_the_Future_of_Montenegro" title="For the Future of Montenegro">For the Future of Montenegro</a>" (ZBCG) <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_group" title="Parliamentary group">parliamentary group</a>, composed mainly of Serb nationalist parties, formed a government under Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Zdravko_Krivokapi%C4%87" title="Zdravko Krivokapić">Zdravko Krivokapic.</a><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> Prime Minister Krivokapic's government was toppled in a no-confidence vote after just 14 months in power.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In April 2022, a new minority government, led by Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87" title="Dritan Abazović">Dritan Abazović</a>, brought together <a href="/wiki/Political_moderate" title="Political moderate">moderate</a> parties that are both pro-European and pro-Serb. However, his government lost a confidence vote after only 113 days. Montenegro had been unable to find a government which could command the confidence of the Parliament,<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Dritan_Abazovi%C4%87" title="Dritan Abazović">Abazović</a> remains in post following the <a href="/wiki/2023_Montenegrin_parliamentary_election" title="2023 Montenegrin parliamentary election">Parliamentary election which took place on 11 June 2023</a> where the <a href="/wiki/Europe_Now!" title="Europe Now!">Europe Now!</a> party led by <a href="/wiki/Jakov_Milatovi%C4%87" title="Jakov Milatović">Jakov Milatović</a> won the most seats, Jakov Milatović having won the <a href="/wiki/2023_Montenegrin_presidential_election" title="2023 Montenegrin presidential election">2023 Montenegrin presidential election</a> and taken office on 20 May 2023. <a href="/wiki/Milojko_Spaji%C4%87" title="Milojko Spajić">Milojko Spajić</a> became prime minister designate in August 2023.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Montenegro" title="Foreign relations of Montenegro">Foreign relations of Montenegro</a></div>
<p>The Ministry of <a href="/wiki/Foreign_Affairs" title="Foreign Affairs">Foreign Affairs</a> was given the task of defining the foreign policy priorities and activities needed for their implementation in cooperation with other state administration authorities, the President, the Speaker of the Parliament, and other relevant stakeholders.<sup id="cite_ref-mvpei.gov.me_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mvpei.gov.me-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The country joined <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> on 5 June 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_Montenegro_to_the_European_Union" title="Accession of Montenegro to the European Union">Integration into the European Union</a> remains a high-priority for Montenegro and has been the focus of Montenegrin foreign policy since its independence from Serbia.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> In June 2023, newly elected President Milatović stated that he expects Montenegro to <a href="/wiki/Accession_of_Montenegro_to_the_European_Union" title="Accession of Montenegro to the European Union">join the European Union</a> by 2027 or 2028.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_and_law_enforcement">Military and law enforcement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Military and law enforcement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Montenegro" title="Armed Forces of Montenegro">Armed Forces of Montenegro</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Final_formation_(7168937179).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Final_formation_%287168937179%29.jpg/220px-Final_formation_%287168937179%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Final_formation_%287168937179%29.jpg/330px-Final_formation_%287168937179%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Final_formation_%287168937179%29.jpg/440px-Final_formation_%287168937179%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2200" data-file-height="1500" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Montenegro" title="Armed Forces of Montenegro">Montenegrin army soldiers</a> with <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> allies from <a href="/wiki/United_States" title="United States">United States</a>, <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Albania" title="Albania">Albania</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slovenia" title="Slovenia">Slovenia</a> and <a href="/wiki/North_Macedonia" title="North Macedonia">North Macedonia</a> during "Immediate Response 2012", <a href="/wiki/Slunj" title="Slunj">Slunj</a>, <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Montenegro">military of Montenegro</a> is a professional <a href="/wiki/Standing_army" title="Standing army">standing army</a> under the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(Montenegro)" title="Ministry of Defence (Montenegro)">Ministry of Defence</a>. It is composed of the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_Ground_Army" title="Montenegrin Ground Army">Montenegrin Ground Army</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_Navy" title="Montenegrin Navy">Montenegrin Navy</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_Air_Force" title="Montenegrin Air Force">Montenegrin Air Force</a>, along with <a href="/wiki/Special_forces" title="Special forces">special forces</a>. Conscription was abolished in 2006. The military maintains an active duty force of 2,400. The bulk of its equipment and forces were inherited from the armed forces of the State Union of <a href="/wiki/Serbia_and_Montenegro" title="Serbia and Montenegro">Serbia and Montenegro</a>. Montenegro contained the entire <a href="/wiki/Coastline" class="mw-redirect" title="Coastline">coastline</a> of the former union and retained practically the entire naval force.
</p><p>Montenegro is a NATO member<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> and a member of <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Charter" title="Adriatic Charter">Adriatic Charter</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> The government planned to have the army participate in <a href="/wiki/Peacekeeping" title="Peacekeeping">peacekeeping</a> missions through the UN and NATO such as the <a href="/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force" title="International Security Assistance Force">International Security Assistance Force</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Montenegro" title="Law enforcement in Montenegro">Civil law enforcement in Montenegro</a> is primarily the responsibility of the Police Directorate, the national police force. <a href="/wiki/Municipal_police" title="Municipal police">Municipal police</a>, known as Communal Police in Montenegro, enforce local laws in their respective municipalities.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Symbols">Symbols</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Symbols">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/National_symbols_of_Montenegro" title="National symbols of Montenegro">National symbols of Montenegro</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg/220px-Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg/330px-Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg/440px-Njego%C5%A1-Mausoleum.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3968" data-file-height="2976" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Mausoleum_of_Njego%C5%A1" title="Mausoleum of Njegoš">Mausoleum</a> of <a href="/wiki/Petar_II_Petrovi%C4%87-Njego%C5%A1" title="Petar II Petrović-Njegoš">Petar II Petrović-Njegoš</a> in <a href="/wiki/Lov%C4%87en_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Lovćen National Park">Lovćen</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The flag includes a border and arms in gold and a golden lion in the center.
</p><p>The national day of <a href="/wiki/Statehood_Day_(Montenegro)" title="Statehood Day (Montenegro)">13 July</a> marks the date in 1878 when the <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_Berlin" title="Congress of Berlin">Congress of Berlin</a> recognised Montenegro as the 27th independent state in the world<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> and the start of one of the first popular uprisings in Europe against the <a href="/wiki/Axis_Powers" class="mw-redirect" title="Axis Powers">Axis Powers</a> on 13 July 1941 in Montenegro.
</p><p>In 2004, the Montenegrin legislature selected a popular Montenegrin traditional song, "<a href="/wiki/Oj,_svijetla_majska_zoro" title="Oj, svijetla majska zoro">Oh, Bright Dawn of May</a>", as the <a href="/wiki/National_anthem" title="National anthem">national anthem</a>. Montenegro's official anthem during the reign of King Nicholas I was <i><a href="/wiki/Ubavoj_nam_Crnoj_Gori" title="Ubavoj nam Crnoj Gori">Ubavoj nam Crnoj Gori</a></i> ("To Our Beautiful Montenegro").
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Montenegro" title="Municipalities of Montenegro">Municipalities of Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_regions_of_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="List of regions of Montenegro">List of regions of Montenegro</a></div>
<p>Montenegro is divided into twenty-four municipalities (<i><a href="/wiki/Op%C5%A1tina" title="Opština">opština</a></i>). This includes 21 District-level Municipalities and 2 Urban Municipalities, with two subdivisions of <a href="/wiki/Municipality_of_Podgorica" class="mw-redirect" title="Municipality of Podgorica">Podgorica municipality</a>, listed below.  Each municipality can contain multiple cities and towns.  Historically, the territory of the country was divided into "nahije".
</p><p>Regions of Montenegro—designed for statistical purposes by the Statistical Office—have no administrative function. Note that other organizations (i.e. <a href="/wiki/Football_Association_of_Montenegro" title="Football Association of Montenegro">Football Association of Montenegro</a>) use different municipalities as a part of similar regions.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg/420px-Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg.png" decoding="async" width="420" height="508" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg/630px-Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg/840px-Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1063" data-file-height="1285" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Montenegro" title="Municipalities of Montenegro">Municipalities</a> and <a href="/wiki/Statistical_regions_of_Montenegro" title="Statistical regions of Montenegro">Statistical regions</a> of Montenegro</figcaption></figure>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Montenegro" title="Economy of Montenegro">Economy of Montenegro</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Second_serie_5%2C_10%2C_20%2C_50_Euro_banknotes.jpg/300px-Second_serie_5%2C_10%2C_20%2C_50_Euro_banknotes.jpg 1.5x, data-file-height="3448" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">The de facto <a href="/wiki/Montenegro_and_the_euro" title="Montenegro and the euro">currency of Montenegro is the euro</a>.</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Montenegro_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Montenegro_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/300px-Montenegro_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Montenegro_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/400px-Montenegro_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">A proportional representation of Montenegro exports, 2019.</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The economy of Montenegro is mostly <a href="/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Tertiary sector of the economy">service-based</a> and is in <a href="/wiki/Transition_economy" title="Transition economy">late transition</a> to a <a href="/wiki/Market_economy" title="Market economy">market economy</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Nominal_GDP" class="mw-redirect" title="Nominal GDP">nominal GDP</a> of Montenegro was $5.424&#160;billion in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-WEO2019_6-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WEO2019-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">GDP PPP</a> for 2019 was $12.516&#160;billion, or $20,083 per capita.<sup id="cite_ref-WEO2019_6-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WEO2019-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/Eurostat" title="Eurostat">Eurostat</a> data, the Montenegrin GDP per capita stood at 48% of the EU average in 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Montenegro" title="Central Bank of Montenegro">Central Bank of Montenegro</a> is not part of the euro system but the country is "<a href="/wiki/Euroisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Euroisation">euroised</a>", using <a href="/wiki/Montenegro_and_the_euro" title="Montenegro and the euro">the euro unilaterally</a> as its currency.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Infrastructure">Infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Montenegro" title="Transport in Montenegro">Transport in Montenegro</a></div>
<p>The Montenegrin road infrastructure is not at Western European standards. No roads meet full motorway standards. Construction of new motorways is considered a national priority, as they are important for uniform economic development and the development of Montenegro as an attractive tourist destination.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Montenegro_motorways.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Montenegro_motorways.JPG/200px-Montenegro_motorways.JPG" decoding="async" width="200" height="269" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Montenegro_motorways.JPG/300px-Montenegro_motorways.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Montenegro_motorways.JPG/400px-Montenegro_motorways.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1453" data-file-height="1953" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">Roads of Montenegro in service and planned</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg/200px-A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="135" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg/300px-A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg/400px-A-1_motorway_in_Montenegro.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3079" data-file-height="2072" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/A-1_motorway_(Montenegro)" title="A-1 motorway (Montenegro)">A-1 motorway</a>, part of the larger international project between Montenegro and neighboring <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a> that connects to the Belgrade–Bar motorway</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/European_route" class="mw-redirect" title="European route">European routes</a> that pass through Montenegro are <a href="/wiki/European_route_E65" title="European route E65">E65</a> and <a href="/wiki/European_route_E80" title="European route E80">E80</a>.
</p><p>The backbone of the Montenegrin rail network is the <a href="/wiki/Belgrade%E2%80%93Bar_railway" title="Belgrade–Bar railway">Belgrade–Bar railway</a>, which provides international connection towards <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>. A domestic branch line, the <a href="/wiki/Nik%C5%A1i%C4%87-Podgorica_railway" class="mw-redirect" title="Nikšić-Podgorica railway">Nikšić-Podgorica railway</a> operated as a freight-only line for decades, that opened for passenger traffic after reconstruction and electrification in 2012. The other branch line from Podgorica towards the Albanian border, the <a href="/wiki/Podgorica%E2%80%93Shkod%C3%ABr_railway" title="Podgorica–Shkodër railway">Podgorica–Shkodër railway</a>, is not in use.
</p><p>Montenegro has two international airports, <a href="/wiki/Podgorica_Airport" title="Podgorica Airport">Podgorica Airport</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tivat_Airport" title="Tivat Airport">Tivat Airport</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Port_of_Bar" title="Port of Bar">Port of Bar</a> is Montenegro's main seaport. Initially built in 1906, the port was almost completely destroyed during World War II. Reconstruction began in 1950. It is equipped to handle over five million tons of cargo annually, but has been operating at a loss and well below capacity. The reconstruction of the <a href="/wiki/Belgrade-Bar_railway" class="mw-redirect" title="Belgrade-Bar railway">Belgrade-Bar railway</a> and the proposed <a href="/wiki/Belgrade-Bar_motorway" class="mw-redirect" title="Belgrade-Bar motorway">Belgrade-Bar motorway</a> are expected to return operating levels to capacity.
</p><p>In 2023 there is a plan to install an <a href="/wiki/LNG" class="mw-redirect" title="LNG">LNG</a> terminal at Bar to receive gas imports.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Montenegro" title="Tourism in Montenegro">Tourism in Montenegro</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:R-1_regional_road_(Montenegro)_07.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/R-1_regional_road_%28Montenegro%29_07.jpg/200px-R-1_regional_road_%28Montenegro%29_07.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/R-1_regional_road_%28Montenegro%29_07.jpg/300px-R-1_regional_road_%28Montenegro%29_07.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/R-1_regional_road_%28Montenegro%29_07.jpg/400px-R-1_regional_road_%28Montenegro%29_07.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5152" data-file-height="3864" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">The <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Kotor" title="Bay of Kotor">Bay of Kotor</a>, a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg/200px-The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="133" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg/300px-The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/56/The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg/400px-The_Black_Lake_in_Montenegro.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3888" data-file-height="2592" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Durmitor" title="Durmitor">Durmitor</a> National Park was founded in 1952, and designated as a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> in 1980</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>A total of 2.1&#160;million visitors visited Montenegro in 2022 spending 12.4m nights there.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> The majority of foreign visitors to Montenegro come from the neighbouring countries of Serbia, Bosnia and Herzegovina and Kosovo, as well as Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>The Montenegrin <a href="/wiki/Adriatic" class="mw-redirect" title="Adriatic">Adriatic</a> coast is 295&#160;km (183&#160;mi) long, with 72&#160;km (45&#160;mi) of beaches and many well-preserved ancient towns. Some of the most popular beaches include <a href="/wiki/Jaz_Beach" title="Jaz Beach">Jaz Beach</a>, <a href="/wiki/Budva" title="Budva">Mogren Beach</a>, <a href="/wiki/Be%C4%8Di%C4%87i" title="Bečići">Bečići Beach</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sveti_Stefan" title="Sveti Stefan">Sveti Stefan Beach</a> and <a href="/wiki/Velika_Pla%C5%BEa" title="Velika Plaža">Velika Plaža</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-My_Guide_Montenegro_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-My_Guide_Montenegro-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Meanwhile, some of the most popular ancient towns include <a href="/wiki/Herceg_Novi" title="Herceg Novi">Herceg Novi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Perast" title="Perast">Perast</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kotor" title="Kotor">Kotor</a>, <a href="/wiki/Budva" title="Budva">Budva</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ulcinj" title="Ulcinj">Ulcinj</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-My_Guide_Montenegro_2020_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-My_Guide_Montenegro_2020-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/National_Geographic_Traveler" title="National Geographic Traveler">National Geographic Traveler</a></i> (edited once a decade) ranks Montenegro among the "50 Places of a Lifetime". Montenegrin seaside town <a href="/wiki/Sveti_Stefan" title="Sveti Stefan">Sveti Stefan</a> was once used as the cover for the magazine.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> The coast region of Montenegro was considered one of the great "discoveries" among world tourists. In January 2010, <i><a href="/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times">The New York Times</a></i> ranked the Ulcinj South Coast region of Montenegro, including <a href="/wiki/Velika_Pla%C5%BEa" title="Velika Plaža">Velika Plaža</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ada_Bojana" title="Ada Bojana">Ada Bojana</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Hotel_Mediteran" title="Hotel Mediteran">Hotel Mediteran</a> of Ulcinj, among the "Top 31 Places to Go in 2010" as part of a worldwide ranking.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Montenegro" title="Demographics of Montenegro">Demographics of Montenegro</a> and <a href="/wiki/Demographic_history_of_Montenegro" title="Demographic history of Montenegro">Demographic history of Montenegro</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_structure">Ethnic structure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Ethnic structure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tnone center"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:541px;max-width:541px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:171px;max-width:171px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:225px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png/169px-Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png" decoding="async" width="169" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png/254px-Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png/338px-Distribution_of_Montenegro_cultural_communities.png 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="4000" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">This map represents the spatial distribution of the different Montenegrine cultural communities, definded on the basis of the self-declared ethnicity, religion and mother tongue according to Census 2011</div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:182px;max-width:182px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:225px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG/180px-MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG" decoding="async" width="180" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG/270px-MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG/360px-MontenegroEthnic2011.PNG 2x" data-file-width="1911" data-file-height="2388" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Ethnic structure of Montenegro by settlements, 2011<sup id="cite_ref-MONSTAT_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MONSTAT-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:182px;max-width:182px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:225px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:MontenegroReligion2011.PNG" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/MontenegroReligion2011.PNG/180px-MontenegroReligion2011.PNG" decoding="async" width="180" height="225" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/MontenegroReligion2011.PNG/270px-MontenegroReligion2011.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/MontenegroReligion2011.PNG/360px-MontenegroReligion2011.PNG 2x" data-file-width="1911" data-file-height="2388" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Religious structure of Montenegro by settlements, 2011<sup id="cite_ref-MONSTAT_119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MONSTAT-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The 2011 census reported 620,029 citizens.<sup id="cite_ref-census2011_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census2011-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> According to the U.S. Census Bureau, in 2015, 9,486 ethnic Serbs there were born in "Other Eastern Europe" countries, overwhelmingly Montenegro.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2000 U.S. census, 2,339 individuals claimed first ancestry in Montenegro, and 189 whose second ancestry was Montenegrin, totaling 2,528 overall.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Montenegro is a <a href="/wiki/Multinational_state" title="Multinational state">multiethnic state</a> with no ethnic majority.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Major ethnic groups include <a href="/wiki/Montenegrins_(ethnic_group)" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrins (ethnic group)">Montenegrins</a> (Црногорци/<i>Crnogorci</i>) and <a href="/wiki/Serbs" title="Serbs">Serbs</a> (Срби/<i>Srbi</i>); others are <a href="/wiki/Bosniaks" title="Bosniaks">Bosniaks</a> (<i>Bošnjaci</i>), <a href="/wiki/Muslims_(ethnic_group)" title="Muslims (ethnic group)">Muslims (ethnic group)</a> (<i>Muslimani</i>), <a href="/wiki/Albanians" title="Albanians">Albanians</a> (<i>Albanci&#160;– Shqiptarët</i>) and <a href="/wiki/Croats" title="Croats">Croats</a> (<i>Hrvati</i>). The number of "Montenegrins" and "Serbs" fluctuates widely from census to census due to changes in how people perceive, experience, or choose to express, their identity and ethnic affiliation.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Montenegro" title="Languages of Montenegro">Languages of Montenegro</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:204px;max-width:204px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png/200px-Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png" decoding="async" width="200" height="119" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png/300px-Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png/400px-Be%C4%8Dko_izdanje_Gorskog_vijenca_1847.png 2x" data-file-width="852" data-file-height="507" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/The_Mountain_Wreath" title="The Mountain Wreath">The Mountain Wreath</a>, poetic chronicle of the struggle of people of Montenegro during <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin%E2%80%93Ottoman_War_(1861%E2%80%931862)" title="Montenegrin–Ottoman War (1861–1862)">Montenegrin–Ottoman War</a>, for freedom and independence from  Ottoman Empire.</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:202px;max-width:202px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Ostrog01.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Ostrog01.JPG/200px-Ostrog01.JPG" decoding="async" width="200" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Ostrog01.JPG/300px-Ostrog01.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/Ostrog01.JPG/400px-Ostrog01.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Ostrog_Monastery" title="Ostrog Monastery">Ostrog</a> Orthodox monastery situated against an almost vertical background, high up in the large rock of Ostroška Greda, it is the most visited religious shrine in the country, which is gladly visited by both Orthodox Christians and Catholics, as well as people of other religions.</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>The official language in Montenegro is <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_language" title="Montenegrin language">Montenegrin</a>. <a href="/wiki/Serbian_language" title="Serbian language">Serbian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_language" title="Bosnian language">Bosnian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Croatian_language" title="Croatian language">Croatian</a> are recognised in official usage. Montenegrin, Serbian, Bosnian, and Croatian are <a href="/wiki/Mutually_intelligible" class="mw-redirect" title="Mutually intelligible">mutually intelligible</a> as <a href="/wiki/Standard_varieties" class="mw-redirect" title="Standard varieties">standard varieties</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Serbo-Croatian_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Serbo-Croatian language">Serbo-Croatian language</a>. Montenegrin is the plurality mother-tongue of the under-18 population.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Previous constitutions endorsed Serbo-Croatian as the official language in <a href="/wiki/SR_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="SR Montenegro">SR Montenegro</a> and Serbian of the <a href="/wiki/Ijekavian" class="mw-redirect" title="Ijekavian">Ijekavian</a> standard during the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Montenegro_(federal)" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of Montenegro (federal)">1992–2006 period</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Montenegro" title="Religion in Montenegro">Religion in Montenegro</a></div>
<p>Montenegro has historically stood at the crossroads of multiculturalism and over centuries this has shaped its unique co-existence between Muslim and Christian populations.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Montenegrins have historically been members of the <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Orthodox_Church" title="Serbian Orthodox Church">Serbian Orthodox Church</a> (governed by the <a href="/wiki/Metropolitanate_of_Montenegro_and_the_Littoral" title="Metropolitanate of Montenegro and the Littoral">Metropolitanate of Montenegro and the Littoral</a>). Serbian Orthodox Christianity is the most popular religion. The <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_Orthodox_Church" title="Montenegrin Orthodox Church">Montenegrin Orthodox Church</a> was recently founded and is followed by a minority of Montenegrins, although it is not in communion with any other canonical Christian Orthodox Church as it has not been officially recognised.
</p><p>Despite tensions between religious groups during the <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_War" title="Bosnian War">Bosnian War</a>, Montenegro remained fairly stable, mainly due to its population's perspective on religious tolerance and faith diversity.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Religious institutions have guaranteed rights and are separate from the state. The second largest religion is <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a>, practiced by 19% of the population. Montenegro has one of the highest proportion of Muslims in Europe and the third highest proportion among Slavic countries, behind only Bosnia and Herzegovina and North Macedonia. A little more than one-fourth of the country's <a href="/wiki/Albanians" title="Albanians">Albanians</a> are <a href="/wiki/Catholics" class="mw-redirect" title="Catholics">Catholics</a> (8,126 in the 2004 census) while the rest (22,267) are mainly <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Muslims" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunni Muslims">Sunni Muslims</a>; in 2012 a protocol recognised <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> as an official religion, which ensures that <a href="/wiki/Halal" title="Halal">halal</a> foods are served at military facilities, hospitals, dormitories and social facilities; and that Muslim women are permitted to wear headscarves in schools and at public institutions, as well as ensuring that Muslims have the right to take Fridays off for the <a href="/wiki/Jumu%27ah" class="mw-redirect" title="Jumu&#39;ah">Jumu'ah</a> (Friday)-prayer.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Since the time of <a href="/wiki/Vojislavljevi%C4%87_dynasty" title="Vojislavljević dynasty">Vojislavljević dynasty</a> Catholicism is autochthonous in the Montenegrin area.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> A small Roman Catholic population, mostly Albanians with some Croats, is divided between the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Bar" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Bar">Archdiocese of Antivari</a> headed by the Primate of Serbia and the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Kotor" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Kotor">Diocese of Kotor</a> that is a part of the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Croatia" title="Catholic Church in Croatia">Catholic Church in Croatia</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Montenegro" title="Culture of Montenegro">Culture of Montenegro</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:224px;max-width:224px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:222px;max-width:222px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladin_Dom_(Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy)_w_Cetinje_01.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Vladin_Dom_%28Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy%29_w_Cetinje_01.jpg/220px-Vladin_Dom_%28Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy%29_w_Cetinje_01.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Vladin_Dom_%28Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy%29_w_Cetinje_01.jpg/330px-Vladin_Dom_%28Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy%29_w_Cetinje_01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Vladin_Dom_%28Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy%29_w_Cetinje_01.jpg/440px-Vladin_Dom_%28Dom_Rz%C4%85dowy%29_w_Cetinje_01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3185" data-file-height="2123" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/National_Museum_of_Montenegro" title="National Museum of Montenegro">National Museum of Montenegro</a></div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:222px;max-width:222px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Tripundan_2023_(04).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Tripundan_2023_%2804%29.jpg/220px-Tripundan_2023_%2804%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Tripundan_2023_%2804%29.jpg/330px-Tripundan_2023_%2804%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Tripundan_2023_%2804%29.jpg/440px-Tripundan_2023_%2804%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="4000" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center">Boka Navy, from 2021 part of intangible <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage" class="mw-redirect" title="World Heritage">World Heritage</a> <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Montenegrin culture has been shaped most importantly by Orthodox, Ottoman (Turk), Slavic, Central European, and seafaring Adriatic cultures (notably parts of Italy, like the <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Venice" title="Republic of Venice">Republic of Venice</a>).
</p><p>Montenegro has many significant cultural and historical sites, including heritage sites from the pre-<a href="/wiki/Romanesque_architecture" title="Romanesque architecture">Romanesque</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gothic_art" title="Gothic art">Gothic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Baroque" title="Baroque">Baroque</a> periods. The Montenegrin coastal region is known for its religious monuments, including the <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_Saint_Tryphon" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathedral of Saint Tryphon">Cathedral of Saint Tryphon</a> in <a href="/wiki/Kotor" title="Kotor">Kotor</a><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> (Cattaro under the Venetians), the basilica of St. Luke (over 800 years), <a href="/wiki/Our_Lady_of_the_Rocks" title="Our Lady of the Rocks">Our Lady of the Rocks</a> (Škrpjela), the <a href="/wiki/Savina_monastery_(Montenegro)" class="mw-redirect" title="Savina monastery (Montenegro)">Savina Monastery</a> and others. Medieval monasteries contain many artistically important frescoes.
</p><p>One cultural dimension is the ethical ideal of <i>Čojstvo i Junaštvo</i>, "Humaneness and Gallantry".<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> The traditional folk dance of the Montenegrins is the <a href="/wiki/Oro_(eagle_dance)" class="mw-redirect" title="Oro (eagle dance)">Oro</a>, the "eagle dance" that involves dancing in circles with couples alternating in the centre, and is finished by forming a human pyramid of dancers standing on each other's shoulders.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Media_of_Montenegro" class="mw-redirect" title="Media of Montenegro">Media of Montenegro</a></div>
<p>Television, magazines, and newspapers are operated by both state-owned and for-profit corporations that depend on advertising, <a href="/wiki/Subscription" class="mw-redirect" title="Subscription">subscription</a>, and other sales-related revenues. The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Montenegro" title="Constitution of Montenegro">Constitution of Montenegro</a> guarantees freedom of speech. Montenegro's media system is under transformation, along with the rest of the country.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sport">Sport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Sport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Montenegro" title="Sport in Montenegro">Sport in Montenegro</a></div>
<table class="wikitable" style="text-align:right; float:left; margin-right:9px; margin-left:2px;">

<tbody><tr>
<td style="text-align:left;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Kovinic_WM17_(14)_(36050694661).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Kovinic_WM17_%2814%29_%2836050694661%29.jpg/130px-Kovinic_WM17_%2814%29_%2836050694661%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="130" height="195" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Kovinic_WM17_%2814%29_%2836050694661%29.jpg/195px-Kovinic_WM17_%2814%29_%2836050694661%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Kovinic_WM17_%2814%29_%2836050694661%29.jpg/260px-Kovinic_WM17_%2814%29_%2836050694661%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2090" data-file-height="3136" /></a></span></td>
<td style="text-align:left;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_(159).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_%28159%29.jpg/130px-Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_%28159%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="130" height="195" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_%28159%29.jpg/195px-Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_%28159%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_%28159%29.jpg/260px-Iran_vs._Montenegro_2014-05-26_%28159%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2000" data-file-height="3000" /></a></span>
</td></tr>
<tr>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Danka_Kovini%C4%87" title="Danka Kovinić">Danka Kovinić</a>,<br />tennis player
</td>
<td style="text-align:center;"><a href="/wiki/Stevan_Joveti%C4%87" title="Stevan Jovetić">Stevan Jovetić</a>,<br />football player
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Sport in Montenegro revolves mostly around <a href="/wiki/Team_sport" title="Team sport">team sports</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Water_polo" title="Water polo">water polo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Handball" title="Handball">handball</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>. Other sports involved are <a href="/wiki/Boxing" title="Boxing">boxing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tennis" title="Tennis">tennis</a>, swimming, <a href="/wiki/Judo" title="Judo">judo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Karate" title="Karate">karate</a>, <a href="/wiki/Athletics_(sport)" class="mw-redirect" title="Athletics (sport)">athletics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Table_tennis" title="Table tennis">table tennis</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Chess" title="Chess">chess</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Water_polo" title="Water polo">Water polo</a> is the most popular and is considered the <a href="/wiki/National_sport" title="National sport">national sport</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Popular_Sports_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Popular_Sports-136">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Montenegro_men%27s_national_water_polo_team" title="Montenegro men&#39;s national water polo team">Montenegro men's national water polo team</a> is one of the world's <a href="/wiki/FINA_Water_Polo_World_Rankings" title="FINA Water Polo World Rankings">top ranked</a> teams, winning the gold medal at the <a href="/wiki/2008_Men%27s_European_Water_Polo_Championship" title="2008 Men&#39;s European Water Polo Championship">2008 Men's European Water Polo Championship</a> in <a href="/wiki/M%C3%A1laga" title="Málaga">Málaga</a>, Spain, and winning the gold medal at the <a href="/wiki/2009_FINA_Men%27s_Water_Polo_World_League" title="2009 FINA Men&#39;s Water Polo World League">2009 FINA Men's Water Polo World League</a>, held in <a href="/wiki/Podgorica" title="Podgorica">Podgorica</a>. The Montenegrin team <a href="/wiki/PVK_Primorac" class="mw-redirect" title="PVK Primorac">PVK Primorac</a> from <a href="/wiki/Kotor" title="Kotor">Kotor</a> became a champion of Europe at the <a href="/wiki/LEN_Euroleague" class="mw-redirect" title="LEN Euroleague">LEN Euroleague</a> 2009 in <a href="/wiki/Rijeka" title="Rijeka">Rijeka</a>, Croatia. Montenegro came fourth in the men's water polo in the <a href="/wiki/Water_polo_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics" title="Water polo at the 2016 Summer Olympics">2016 Olympics</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the second most popular sport.<sup id="cite_ref-Popular_Sports_136-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Popular_Sports-136">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Notable players are <a href="/wiki/Dejan_Savi%C4%87evi%C4%87" title="Dejan Savićević">Dejan Savićević</a>, <a href="/wiki/Predrag_Mijatovi%C4%87" title="Predrag Mijatović">Predrag Mijatović</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mirko_Vu%C4%8Dini%C4%87" title="Mirko Vučinić">Mirko Vučinić</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stefan_Savi%C4%87" title="Stefan Savić">Stefan Savić</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stevan_Joveti%C4%87" title="Stevan Jovetić">Stevan Jovetić</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Stefan_Mugo%C5%A1a" title="Stefan Mugoša">Stefan Mugoša</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_national_football_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin national football team">Montenegrin national football team</a>, founded in 2006, played in playoffs for <a href="/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2012" title="UEFA Euro 2012">UEFA Euro 2012</a>, its biggest success. The <a href="/wiki/Montenegro_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegro national basketball team">Montenegro national basketball team</a> is known for good performances and won a lot of medals as part of the <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavia_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Yugoslavia national basketball team">Yugoslavia national basketball team</a>. In 2006, the <a href="/wiki/Basketball_Federation_of_Montenegro" title="Basketball Federation of Montenegro">Basketball Federation of Montenegro</a> along with this team joined the <a href="/wiki/International_Basketball_Federation" class="mw-redirect" title="International Basketball Federation">International Basketball Federation</a> (FIBA) on its own, following the <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_independence_referendum,_2006" class="mw-redirect" title="Montenegrin independence referendum, 2006">Independence</a>. Montenegro participated in two <a href="/wiki/EuroBasket" title="EuroBasket">EuroBaskets</a>.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MontenegrinFans2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/MontenegrinFans2.jpg/220px-MontenegrinFans2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/MontenegrinFans2.jpg/330px-MontenegrinFans2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/MontenegrinFans2.jpg/440px-MontenegrinFans2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="408" /></a><figcaption>Football fans at <a href="/wiki/Podgorica_City_Stadium" title="Podgorica City Stadium">Podgorica City Stadium</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Among women sports, <a href="/wiki/Montenegro_women%27s_national_handball_team" title="Montenegro women&#39;s national handball team">the national handball team</a> is the most successful, winning the country's first Olympic medal, claiming silver at the <a href="/wiki/Handball_at_the_2012_Summer_Olympics" title="Handball at the 2012 Summer Olympics">2012 Summer Olympics</a>. This was followed by the <a href="/wiki/2012_European_Women%27s_Handball_Championship" title="2012 European Women&#39;s Handball Championship">2012 European Championship</a> which Montenegro won, becoming European champions. <a href="/wiki/%C5%BDRK_Budu%C4%87nost_Podgorica" title="ŽRK Budućnost Podgorica">ŽRK Budućnost Podgorica</a> has twice won the EHF Champions League. Montenegro was one of the host countries for the <a href="/wiki/2022_European_Women%27s_Handball_Championship" title="2022 European Women&#39;s Handball Championship">2022 European Women's Handball Championship</a> and came third.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Chess" title="Chess">Chess</a> is another popular sport and notable global chess players such as <a href="/wiki/Slavko_Dedi%C4%87" title="Slavko Dedić">Slavko Dedić</a> were born there.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Montenegrin_cuisine" title="Montenegrin cuisine">Montenegrin cuisine</a></div>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg/290px-Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="180" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg/435px-Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg/580px-Foods_from_Montenegro.jpg 2x" data-file-width="626" data-file-height="389" /></a><figcaption>Foods from Montenegro.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The first major influences to Montenegrin cuisine came from the <a href="/wiki/Levant" title="Levant">Levant</a> and <a href="/wiki/Turkish_cuisine" title="Turkish cuisine">Turkey</a>: <a href="/wiki/Sarma_(food)" title="Sarma (food)">sarma</a>, <a href="/wiki/Moussaka" title="Moussaka">musaka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pilaf" title="Pilaf">pilav</a>, <a href="/wiki/B%C3%B6rek#Balkans" title="Börek">pita</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gibanica" title="Gibanica">gibanica</a>, <a href="/wiki/Burek" class="mw-redirect" title="Burek">burek</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C4%86evapi" title="Ćevapi">ćevapi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kebab" title="Kebab">kebab</a>, <a href="/wiki/%C4%90uve%C4%8D" class="mw-redirect" title="Đuveč">đuveč</a>, and Turkish sweets such as <a href="/wiki/Baklava" title="Baklava">baklava</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tulumba" title="Tulumba">tulumba</a>. <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_cuisine" title="Hungarian cuisine">Hungarian cuisine</a> influences <a href="/wiki/Goulash" title="Goulash">stews</a> and <a href="/wiki/Satara%C5%A1" title="Sataraš">sataraš</a>. While <a href="/wiki/Central_European_cuisine" title="Central European cuisine">Central European cuisine</a> is evident in the prevalence of <a href="/wiki/Cr%C3%AApes" class="mw-redirect" title="Crêpes">crêpes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Doughnuts" class="mw-redirect" title="Doughnuts">doughnuts</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jam" class="mw-redirect" title="Jam">jams</a>, many types of <a href="/wiki/Biscuits" class="mw-redirect" title="Biscuits">biscuits</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cakes" class="mw-redirect" title="Cakes">cakes</a>, and various kinds of breads. Montenegrin cuisine also varies geographically; with the cuisine in the coastal area differing from that of the northern highland region. The coastal area is traditionally a representative of <a href="/wiki/Mediterranean_cuisine" title="Mediterranean cuisine">Mediterranean cuisine</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Seafood" title="Seafood">seafood</a> being a common dish. The traditional dishes of Montenegro's <a href="/wiki/Adriatic_Sea" title="Adriatic Sea">Adriatic coast</a>, unlike its heartland, have been significantly influenced by <a href="/wiki/Italian_cuisine" title="Italian cuisine">Italian</a> cuisine.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Explanatory_notes">Explanatory notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Montenegro&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Explanatory notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-native_name_1-10"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-native_name_1_10-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Albanian_language" title="Albanian language">Albanian</a>: <i lang="sq">Mali i Zi</i>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-native_name_2-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-native_name_2_11-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Written identically in <a href="/wiki/Bosnian_language" title="Bosnian language">Bosnian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Croatian_language" title="Croatian language">Croatian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Serbian_language" title="Serbian language">Serbian</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-native_name_3-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-native_name_3_12-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Written identically in <a href="/wiki/Serbian_Cyrillic_alphabet" title="Serbian Cyrillic alphabet">Serbian Cyrillic</a>.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

</div></td></tr></tbody></table><div></div></td></tr><tr><td class="navbox-abovebelow" colspan="3"><div><div class="hlist"><ul><li><sup>1</sup> Spans the conventional boundary between Europe and another continent.</li><li><sup>2</sup> Considered European for cultural, political and historical reasons but is geographically in Western Asia.</li><li><sup>3</sup> <a href="/wiki/Island#Oceanic_islands" title="Island">Oceanic islands</a> within the vicinity of Europe are usually grouped with the continent even though they are not situated on its continental shelf.</li><li><sup>4</sup> Governed by the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> which has sovereignty over Vatican City.</li></ul></div>
